## CHECKPOINT for next session (2026-05-22 — Phase 3A.c.3 REVERTED; re-approach with diagnostic findings)

**Current HEAD**: `71217ba9` (Phase 3A.c.2 docs backfill). Phase 3A.c.3 implementation was attempted, hit an architectural conflict, was reverted (working tree only — no commit landed for 3A.c.3). Working tree: typing-propagators.rkt restored to 3A.c.2 baseline; propagator.rkt + tests/* retain 3A.c.1's cell-17 cascade (those commits stay landed).

**State**: Full suite **8246 tests / 103.7s / 0 failures** at current working tree (~3A.c.2 baseline; -5 from 3A.c.2 close suspected to be batch-worker test counter noise, all-pass status unaffected).

**Branch**: `main` (ahead of origin/main by 33 commits; don't push unless directed).

### What was attempted in 3A.c.3 (REVERTED — preserved here for next session)

Per §9.3.5.6 + §9.3.5.7 row 3A.c.3, the implementation had two parts:

**Part A (centralized invocation)**: Added single-line `install-classifier-watcher` invocation at the TOP of `install-typing-network`'s inner `let install` recursion (line 2234+). Per β.1 universal install from Decision 1 — every expression position visited by the recursion gets a classifier-watcher propagator.

**Part B (handler completion)**: Added `(net-cell-write n4 decomposed-positions-cell-id (seteq position))` as Step 5 of `process-fork-on-union` handler after the watcher install for/fold. The FP3 guard cell write that 3A.c.2's helper reads.

### The architectural conflict surfaced

Full suite with Part A + Part B applied: **11 failures**, ALL "Could not infer type" errors for polymorphic trait dispatch (filter, reduce, take, drop, into) — implicit type metas remain unresolved.

Per the diagnostic protocol (workflow.md § "Diagnostic protocol when blocked"), STOP and audit:

| Test | Configuration | Result |
|---|---|---|
| Baseline (3A.c.2 state) | NO Part A; NO Part B | All tests PASS |
| Part B only (Part A reverted) | NO Part A; YES Part B | All tests PASS — Part B is harmless on its own (handler doesn't run without watcher emissions) |
| H1 narrowing (skip meta positions) | Watcher install at ALL positions EXCEPT expr-meta | **FAILS** — 2/14 in test-collection-fns-01 |
| Watcher at expr-ann ONLY | Skip everything except expr-ann | **PASSES** — 14/14 |
| Watcher at expr-ann + expr-meta | Skip compound expressions | **PASSES** — 14/14 |

### Diagnostic conclusion

**The issue is watcher install at COMPOUND expression positions** (expr-app, expr-lam, expr-Pi, expr-reduce, expr-Sigma, etc. — the cases where the typing-rule infrastructure or make-app-fire-fn-style propagators write to e's :type computed from child types).

H1 (skip meta positions only) failed → not specifically meta vs non-meta
H4-narrow (install at expr-ann only) passed → install at expr-ann is fine
expr-ann + expr-meta passed → install at meta is also fine (residuation propagator coexists OK)
**Bisection-implied root cause**: SOME compound expression case interacts badly with the classifier-watcher's presence. Specifically the polymorphic trait dispatch tests use heavily-curried function applications (`[filter Nat ?meta22716 ?meta22717 ?meta22721 test::pos? '[0N 1N 2N 0N 3N]]`) — many expr-app sub-expressions.

### Hypotheses to investigate next session

| Hypothesis | Specific mechanism | Diagnostic next step |
|---|---|---|
| **H-compound-1** | Watcher at expr-app's e position adds a propagator on e's :type, but make-app-fire-fn ALSO writes to e's :type computed from child types. The extra propagator on :type may interact badly with BSP scheduling — perhaps the typing-rule propagator's expected fire order breaks | Bisect: test installs at expr-ann + expr-meta + (specific compound case at a time). Find the FIRST compound case that fails |
| **H-compound-2** | Watcher fires on intermediate :type writes during multi-round elaboration. For polymorphic dispatch, types refine progressively. If watcher's presence delays or interferes with trait-resolution timing... | Add instrumentation: count watcher fires per position; check correlation with failing positions |
| **H-compound-3** | `install-from-rule`'s recursive `install-typing-network` calls (line 1912) create FRESH install-typing-network entries for child expressions. The watcher install happens AGAIN at child positions. May cause DUPLICATE watcher installs at the same position if the parent's recursion already installed one | Check: do polymorphic tests' compound expressions trigger install-from-rule's child recursion? If yes, dedup check; if no, hypothesis disconfirmed |
| **H-compound-4** | Fuel-related: TYPING-FUEL-LIMIT = 200. Extra watcher fires may push fuel pressure | Increase TYPING-FUEL-LIMIT to 1000 temporarily; re-test failing tests |
| **H-compound-5** | Order of propagator install matters. Watcher is installed BEFORE the case's specific propagators (line 2239+). For compound cases, the case may install MULTIPLE propagators that depend on each other; watcher placement may break dependency order | Try installing watcher AFTER match dispatch (post-case install). Risks: need access to net post-case |
| **H-compound-6** | Some specific compound case has propagators that READ e's :type and behave differently if there's a co-dependent propagator | Read each compound case's body; identify which ones write/read e's :type |

### Architectural reflection — what this means for Decision 1

**Decision 1 aggregated 10+ principles favoring β.1 with zero principles favoring β.3.** The aggregation was internally consistent. But **β.1 has hit empirical conflict** with existing typing-propagator infrastructure at compound expression cases.

This is the principle-vs-reality conflict the diagnostic protocol exists for. Per workflow.md:
> "When stuck after 2-3 attempts, STOP iterating... Step 4 (Challenge by principles): am I working around a deeper issue?"
> "Step 5 (Reframe if still stuck): question the problem framing itself."

**Possible reframings for next session**:

| Reframing | Description |
|---|---|
| **R1 — Targeted β with semantic distinction** | β.1's "universal install" was idealized; the principled choice may actually be β with semantic-driven scope: install at positions where union classifiers can ARRIVE (expr-ann + expr-meta when constraints flow in). Skip compound cases where the typing rule infrastructure already manages :type. NOT case enumeration; SEMANTIC distinction |
| **R2 — Investigate root cause for true β.1** | Maybe β.1 IS the right answer but the implementation pattern needs adjustment. Find the specific compound case + mechanism that breaks; fix it; restore β.1 universality |
| **R3 — Defer 3A.c entirely** | Maybe 3A.c.3 reveals that the on-network mechanism's user-facing wire-up needs more design work. Defer to a fresh design cycle that accounts for the compound-expression conflict |

### Session 2026-05-22 (continued) — Phase 3A.c.5 test-speculation-bridge migration (commit `77ea36e4`)

Per Stage 4 Per-Phase Protocol. Per §9.3.5.6 design intent: ~30-50 LoC assertion updates verifying R7's non-committing classifier preservation in test-speculation-bridge.rkt.

**Audit + implementation**:

Suite 3 (Union Type Speculation; 4 cases):
- left-succeeds: ADD `string-contains? "Nat | Bool"` substring assertion
- left-fails-right-succeeds: same (Nat narrows via S(-1); Bool survives; classifier preserves)
- nested union (A | B) | C: same (R7 mechanism applies recursively)
- both fail: PRESERVED (error-path test; goes through typing-errors.rkt:78 — Parent Phase 4 territory)

Suite 4 (Map Widening; 2 cases):
- union value-type: ADD substring assertion
- no widening: PRESERVED (no union in test)

Suite 6 (Pipeline Integration; 2 cases):
- union types + polymorphic id: ADD substring assertion
- multi-def with union: same

UNCHANGED per §9.3.5.6:
- Suite 5 (QTT union speculation) — qtt.rkt:2329; Parent Phase 4 territory
- Suite 7 (Error Improvement / union exhaustion) — typing-errors.rkt:78; kept alive per §9.3.5.4

Net: 6 active migrations + 2 preserves = 8 union cases addressed (matches §9.3.5.6's "8-10 union test cases" estimate).

Each migrated test KEEPS existing `prologos-error?` assertion (preserves coverage equivalence per §9.3.5.6) AND ADDS `string-contains?` substring assertion verifying union substring in result-str. The substring check is the **LOAD-BEARING DISCRIMINATOR** vs sexp first-success commit:
- Sexp first-success: `(def x <Nat | Bool> 42N)` would commit x's type to Nat → result "x : Nat defined." → substring "Nat | Bool" ABSENT → assertion FAILS
- R7 non-committing: classifier preserves → result "x : Nat | Bool defined." → substring PRESENT → assertion PASSES

**Verification**: 27/27 test-speculation-bridge tests PASS; full suite **8255 / 117.8s / 0 failures**. Adversarial 3-column VAG passed all 4 questions.

**Methodology data point — codification candidate reinforcement (3rd application site this session)**:

"**Multi-axis parity testing for discriminating coverage**" — now has 3 application sites in current session arc:
1. PPN 4C 2A.c orchestration-parity (3 axes triangulating BSP value-tier behavior)
2. PPN 4C 3A.c.4 union-inhabitation parity axes (4 axes; multi-success load-bearing)
3. PPN 4C 3A.c.5 speculation-bridge substring discriminators (6 migrations adding substring assertions atop preserved prologos-error? checks)

The pattern generalizes beyond parity-test framework specifically — it applies to ANY test framework where assertions must DISCRIMINATE architectural variants. **The principle is**: when an architectural change introduces a new semantic property (here: non-committing union inhabitation), tests must include assertions that would FAIL under the OLD variant. Single assertions like "no error" preserve coverage equivalence but don't discriminate. The discriminating assertions are what empirically PROVE the new semantics work.

**Promotion candidate to DEVELOPMENT_LESSONS.org**: 3 application sites in one session is strong evidence; codification should refine the existing "parity-test as design artifact" entry to include this broader pattern.

### Phase 3B preview (per §9.4)

Hypercube integration leveraging already-implemented primitives. Per audit §3.5:

1. **Gray-code branch ordering**: replace naive branch enumeration in process-fork-on-union with `gray-code-order` from relations.rkt. Benefit: successive forks differ by one assumption bit → CHAMP structural sharing maximized.

2. **Subcube pruning on contradictions**: when branch X contradicts, writes nogood; subsequent branches containing the same nogood-bits skipped via `subcube-member?` check (already implemented in decision-cell.rkt:368).

3. **Tests**: performance + correctness (structural sharing benefit measurable via heartbeat counters).

This is INTEGRATION work — the primitives exist; need to wire them into Phase 3A's mechanism. Scope should be smaller than 3A.c (~50-100 LoC).

**Key design questions for Phase 3B mini-design**:
- Where does gray-code-order integrate into process-fork-on-union's for/fold (lines 1135 + 1158)?
- Where does subcube-member? get called (handler? branch check fire-fn?)
- What's the relationship between gray-code-order's bit-ordering and worldview-cache's bit assignment from solver-state-amb?
- What test additions verify structural sharing benefit (heartbeat counters? per-test-case CHAMP allocs?)

### Critical context to retain

1. **R7 architectural state**: union-detection lives INLINE in `type-map-write` (typing-propagators.rkt:585) via `maybe-emit-fork-on-union-request` helper. ANY future track that needs union-detection at a new write site picks it up FOR FREE if writing through type-map-write. Phase 9b γ + Phase 7 trait dispatch + Track 4D attribute grammar rules all inherit.

2. **cell-17 (decomposed-positions-cell-id) infrastructure** is the FP3 idempotence guard. R7's helper reads it BEFORE emit; handler writes it after decomposition. Structural idempotence (Correct by Construction).

3. **Realization B (in-place worldview tagging on shared carrier)** is the architectural choice. Bridges-cause-tag-collapse (Realization A) was the failure mode at 3A.b first attempt. The lazy `promote-cell-to-tagged` at fork-on-union entry IS the load-bearing step that enables per-branch isolation.

4. **Honest scaffolding labels**: elab-speculation-bridge.rkt remains alive for 4 with-speculative-rollback callers (qtt.rkt:2329, typing-errors.rkt:78, possibly 2 more). Labeled scaffolding with Parent Phase 4 / PM 12 retirement plan. NOT preserved "for future use" — preserved because the callers haven't retired yet.

5. **3-column adversarial framing is methodology discipline now**. Apply at every VAG / Mantra audit / Principles gate / P/R/M/S lens. The adversarial column forces rejection-attempt framing that catalogue + challenge alone miss. Validated empirically at 7+ application sites this session.

### Session 2026-05-22 (continued) — Phase 3B mini-design + mini-audit persisted (commit `5df6745a`)

Fresh session opened post-CHECKPOINT per hot-load protocol. Hot-loaded HANDOFF_PROTOCOL.org + DESIGN_METHODOLOGY.org (Stage 3-4) + DESIGN_PRINCIPLES.org (10 principles + Hyperlattice Conjecture + Cell/Propagator/Scheduler Orthogonality) + CRITIQUE_METHODOLOGY.org (3-column adversarial framing). Confirmed methodology discipline; reviewed §9.3.1 Phase 3 mini-design + §9.3.9 Phase 3A cross-arc VAG + §9.4 original terse spec + audit §3.5 (hypercube primitives) + research doc 2026-04-08 (Q_n hypercube + Gray code + bitmask subcube). User direction: audit prior art + research before any Phase 3B design work.

**Audit work executed in parallel** (per session opening):

1. Stage 2 audit §3.5 — confirmed all hypercube primitives are IMPLEMENTED. §3.5.6 explicitly states Phase 9B is INTEGRATION not primary implementation.
2. Hypercube research foundational claims — Q_n = worldview lattice; Gray code = Hamiltonian path; subcube membership O(1) via `(wv AND ng) == ng`.
3. `gray-code-order` source (relations.rkt:1874-1883) — IMPLEMENTED, 3+ test cases, **ZERO production callers** (only test references).
4. `subcube-member?` source (decision-cell.rkt:372-373) — IMPLEMENTED, **ZERO production callers** (only definition + provide; companion `hamming-distance`, `hasse-adjacent?`, `popcount` similarly orphaned).
5. `process-fork-on-union` integration sites (typing-propagators.rkt:1120-1219) — two nested `for/fold` loops; iterate in allocation order; no Gray-code wiring.
6. Mempalace semantic search — `atms-amb-on-network` (BSP-LE 2 D.6 intent) DOES NOT EXIST in current code (replaced by `solver-state-amb`); Gray-code CHAMP-sharing argument is **mechanism-coupled to fork-and-rejoin** (Realization A); Phase 3A uses Realization B (no forks).

**CRITICAL FINDING via adversarial pushback from user (point 1 reply)**:

User pushed back on preliminary "defer" lean: per Hyperlattice Conjecture, Hasse diagram IS optimal structure — Gray code traversal helps realize this REGARDLESS of fork mechanism. Pushback prompted re-audit of `solver-amb` body (atms.rkt:300-320).

**Discovery**: `solver-amb` (atms.rkt:302 comment + 313-319 body) **writes N·(N−1)/2 pairwise mutual-exclusion nogoods** as a side effect of every call. Phase 3A's `process-fork-on-union` invokes `solver-state-amb` → `solver-amb` → pairwise nogoods. **These nogoods exist in the network's nogood store but are NOT consulted by Phase 3A's mechanism** (`wrap-with-worldview`'s single-bit dispatch isolates each branch from observing the global contradiction).

**Architectural debt surfaced**: Phase 3A repurposes `worldview-cache-cell-id` from CLASSICAL ATMS semantic (single point in Q_n; current believed worldview) to ACTIVE-BRANCH-SET semantic (set of viable branches). These are STRUCTURALLY INCOMPATIBLE — under classical interpretation, worldview-cache with multiple bits set is contradiction per the nogoods. Phase 3A's mechanism "works" by structural isolation but is **semantically inconsistent with `solver-amb`'s contract**.

**Pattern**: this is the **3rd "accidentally-load-bearing mechanism" of the addendum** (continuing S2.c-iii `with-handlers` + Path T-3 findings). Phase 3A's mini-design (§9.3.1) missed the side effect because the audit row treated `solver-state-amb` as a contract-less aid allocator. **Capture-gap discipline failure at the mini-audit level**.

**Semantic exploration** (with WebSearch context for Gray code parallel computing + adversarial 3-column on each model):

Three candidate models surfaced:
- **M0** (suppress mutex nogoods at solver-amb call site — minimal patch): adversarial verdict = treats symptom not root cause; safety-patch only, NOT architectural answer
- **M1** (separate active-branch-set cell + new `union-amb` primitive — substrate decomplection): adversarial verdict = real decomplection (structurally-distinct merge contracts); confirmed correct at substrate layer
- **M3** (first-class classifier-set type-theoretic reification — Track 4D scope): adversarial verdict = right but premature; conflates type-system + substrate; belongs in Track 4D's attribute-grammar unification

**Decision (user-confirmed)**: M1 selected as substrate-level answer; M3 deferred to Track 4D; M0 reserved as fallback if M1 too large.

**Phase 3B sub-phase structure refined** (per user confirmation of "3B.0 as measurement phase that feeds back into design considerations" pattern):
- **3B.0** — Measurement + Audit (Pre-0 A/B harness for Gray-code aid ordering + Stage 2 audit A1-A6 for M1 design)

### Session 2026-05-22 (continued) — Phase 3B.0 A1-A6 audit sweep landed (commit `8ca0a1a4`)

A1-A6 all-parallel audit executed per Phase 3B.0 plan. Findings persisted to D.3 §9.4.2.7-§9.4.2.8. **Critical finding surfaced: the audit refutes the strongest framing of "worldview-cache role overload" from §9.4.1 — M0 may actually be the correct minimal architectural fix, not M1.**

**Audit findings**:

- **A1** (solver-state-amb callers): process-fork-on-union (typing-propagators.rkt:1139) is the SOLE non-committing production caller. M1 migration scope: 1 production site.
- **A2** (worldview-cache reads/writes): ~15 callers catalogued. **CRITICAL re-examination**: re-reading `solver-state-with-worldview` (atms.rkt:461-469) shows worldview-cache is structurally "set of currently believed aids" — bitmask shape supports multi-aid sets in BOTH classical + Phase 3A. The "role overload" framing was overstated. The actual incompatibility is ONLY the mutex nogoods.
- **A3** (wrap-with-worldview): ONLY parameterizes current-worldview-bitmask; no worldview-cache reads. Localized change confirmed.
- **A4** (nogood storage consumers): reads only via solver-explain* + solver-state-consistent?. NONE invoked by Phase 3A's process-fork-on-union path. Mutex nogoods are STRUCTURALLY INERT under Phase 3A. M0 safety confirmed.
- **A5** (union-amb primitive design): cell-id 18 slot; per-position hasheq → bitmask shape OR per-position seteq → aid shape; API mirrors solver-state-amb without mutex nogood writes.
- **A6** (process-fork-contradiction under M1) — **DEEPER FINDING**: branch CHECK propagator at typing-propagators.rkt:1173 is NOT registered with `#:assumption aid` (only the WATCHER is, at line 1198-1205). Under M1, moving narrowing to a new cell while keeping tagged-cell-read filter on worldview-cache creates a viability tension. 4 resolution options enumerated (dual-narrowing; assumption-tag branch checks; change tagged-cell-read filter source; accept decomplection-edge).

**Re-evaluation of M0/M1/M3 in light of audit**:

- **M0** (suppress mutex nogoods): **refined assessment** — actually addresses the LOAD-BEARING architectural debt. The "root cause" of role overload was partially mythical. M0 may be the CORRECT minimal fix.
- **M1** (separate active-branch-set cell): **refined assessment** — introduces a NEW architectural tension (branch-check viability vs filter source); decomplection claim weakened. Possibly over-engineered.
- **M3** (Track 4D classifier-set): unchanged; long-term.

**Discourse-turn surfacing**: this is exactly the kind of finding that warrants the "deliberate/discourse turn before confirming implementation approach" the user specified at Phase 3B.0 opening. The audit-driven re-examination should reshape (or confirm) the M1 decision before any code lands. **The methodology is working as designed** — audit informs design.

**Implications for 3B.A scope**:
- If M0: substantially smaller scope (~50 LoC: flag parameter + suppression branch in solver-amb + caller migration)
- If M1: larger scope per original framing (~150-200 LoC: new cell + new primitive + handler migration + branch-check viability resolution)
- If M0 → defer M1 to a future track: explicit M1-not-needed-now decision; codify the "role overload was partially mythical" finding as DEVELOPMENT_LESSONS.org candidate ("rigorous audit refutes initial framing")

**Persisted**:
- D.3 §9.4.2.7 (A1-A4 findings + A1-A4 cross-cutting synthesis with M0/M1 re-examination)
- D.3 §9.4.2.8 (A5-A6 findings including A6 deeper branch-check-viability finding)

**Methodology data points captured**:
1. Audit refutes initial design framing — methodology working ("**rigorous audit refutes initial framing**" codification candidate, 1 data point — watching list)
2. Mini-audit finds deeper-than-expected architectural tensions — codification candidate ("**audit-driven re-examination is honest engineering**" — sister to "audit-driven scope refinement" 4-data-point graduation, 1 data point this session)
3. Adversarial meta-discipline at audit close: M0 might be RIGHT and M1 WRONG; honest framing requires re-opening the decision — not rationalizing prior commitment

**What this falsifies (narrow)**: Gray-code aid ordering provides measurable benefit under Phase 3A's Realization B (in-place worldview tagging on shared carrier).

**What this does NOT falsify (broader)**:
- Hyperlattice Conjecture broadly (general claim about Hasse diagrams)
- CHAMP-sharing under fork-and-rejoin (Realization A; never tested here)
- Future consumer benefit under different mechanism (Phase 9b under fork-and-rejoin, PReduce, BSP-LE 6 — each needs separate per-mechanism A/B when those tracks open)

**Questions worth discussing**:
1. Is the negative result genuinely a falsification, or are we missing a workload that would surface signal?
2. What does this tell us about the Conjecture's mechanism-coupling?
3. What's the broader story for the project — does this affect how we think about Realization B's architectural limits?
4. Are there other places in PPN 4C / future tracks where this finding has direct implications?
5. Should we plan ahead for Phase 9b's mechanism choice (Realization B vs fork-and-rejoin) given this evidence?
6. The 4 codifications graduating to PROMOTION-READY — when do we formally graduate them to DEVELOPMENT_LESSONS.org?

### Critical context to retain

1. **M0 is NOT scaffolding** — it's the architecturally-correct minimal substrate fix at this layer. Track 4D scope absorbs M1+M3 cleanly. Honest framing throughout §9.4.2.9.

2. **Variant B retired honestly** — not "preserved for future use" (workflow.md anti-pattern). Future tracks resurrect from git history (commit `98cbbc3d`). Data preserved at `data/benchmarks/fork-on-union-gray-code-2026-05-22.txt`.

3. **The measurement is empirical falsification, not test inadequacy** — W4 (40 aids) + W5 (35 aids varied widths) explicitly targeted Conjecture's predicted benefit zone. Tight IQRs (~1-2%) leave no room for "noise dominated signal" rationalization.

4. **Audit-driven re-examination IS the methodology working** — §9.4.1 initially committed to M1; §9.4.2.9 reversed it per audit findings. The design doc preserves BOTH (historical M1 commitment + audit-driven re-examination) — honest documentation, not retroactive rewriting.

5. **Pre-committed falsification criteria eliminate motivated reasoning** — thresholds were set at §9.4.1.6 BEFORE measurement. ±5% criterion applied mechanically; no "maybe at larger scale it would help" rationalization possible.

6. **4 codifications PROMOTION-READY** (from this arc):
   - "Rigorous audit refutes initial framing"
   - "Multi-consumer architectural symmetry justifies minimal-fix-now + unification-later"
   - "Pre-committed falsification criteria enable honest negative findings"
   - "Mechanism-coupled architectural claims need mechanism-specific falsification"
   
   These extend the codification capital from Phase 3A's 3 graduation-ready entries. Decision on whether to graduate to `DEVELOPMENT_LESSONS.org` is deferred to user direction.

7. **Multi-consumer architectural symmetry** (audit finding from §9.4.2.9.1): Phase 3A + Phase 7 (parametric trait resolution) + Phase 9b (γ hole-fill) + PReduce 1 (e-class extraction) + BSP-LE 6 (general residual solver) all share the at-least-one + non-committing pattern. M0 serves all 5; Track 4D's M1+M3 unification serves all 5 in the future. This symmetry is load-bearing for the architectural decision.

8. **Phase 9b mechanism question is OPEN** — currently designed to use Realization B per §6.2.1, inheriting Phase 3A's mechanism. If Phase 9b's design re-considers fork-and-rejoin for multi-candidate, the Gray-code test would need to be re-run under that mechanism. Future-track concern.

Atomic code-level migration per §9.4.3.3 sub-step partition (1+2+3 merged for API coupling — each sub-step breaks the next's contract). Implements the M0 substrate fix.

- `solver-amb` (line 316): added `#:mutual-exclusion?` keyword, default `#t`. Step 2 (pairwise mutex nogood loop) made conditional via `cond` — under `#t` writes N·(N−1)/2 nogoods as before; under `#f` skips the loop entirely.
- `solver-state-amb` (line 452): threads flag through. Per Q2(b): amb-groups append ALSO conditional — under `#f`, the new amb-group entry is NOT appended (keeps solve-all semantically correct).
- Documentation: condensed the Variant B retirement note (3B.0 historical record) into 5 lines + forward pointer to §9.4.3 for full M0 architectural rationale. Full M0 doc block lands at 3B.A.4 next.

- `process-fork-on-union` (line 1139): migrated to pass `#:mutual-exclusion? #f`. 7-line comment block explains: union-type inhabitation is at-least-one (non-committing); classical mutex nogoods would be semantically wrong and were structurally inert under the prior code path (per §9.4.3.1 A2).

- Acceptance file: 0 errors

- Compilation clean

- Post-3B.A: under `#:mutual-exclusion? #f`, mutex nogood loop SKIPPED entirely; `solver-context-nogoods-cid` accumulator no longer grows from Phase 3A path; amb-groups doesn't grow either
- Observable counters (PERF + PROVENANCE) unchanged because `solver-add-nogood` never bumped `atms_nogood_count` (verified earlier)

### Session 2026-05-22 (continued) — Phase 3C.a foundation landed (commit `1d803ed2`)

**Phase 3C.a CLOSED** — first sub-phase of Phase 3C delivered. Mini-design persisted at §9.5.2; implementation + tests in same commit per atomic-sub-phase pattern. Foundation ready for 3C.b/c/d consumers.

**Conversational arc this sub-phase**:
1. User opened 3C.a; requested mini-design + mini-audit + conversational deliberation check-in
2. Mini-audit verified API surface (champ-fold + propagator struct accessors + net-cell-read-raw + tagged-cell-value + make-prop-network)
3. Mini-design proposed Q-A.1 through Q-A.7 with 3-column adversarial framing per Q
4. User accepted all leans
5. §9.5.2 persistence (mini-design + mini-audit + Q-A.* locked + test plan + drift risks)
6. Implementation: error-explanation.rkt (156 LoC) + test-error-explanation.rkt (~165 LoC)
7. Initial test failure — srcloc arity mismatch (used Racket's built-in 5-field srcloc; Prologos uses 4-field file/line/col/span). Fixed by re-importing from "../source-location.rkt" and dropping position field from test srclocs.
8. Targeted tests: 9 PASS (~0.0s reported)
9. Full suite regression gate: **8251 tests / 98.8s / 0 failures** (+9 tests vs baseline 8242/111.4s)
10. Single atomic commit `1d803ed2` containing §9.5.2 + impl + tests

**Implementation highlights**:

- `derivation-chain` struct: 1 field (`steps`); transparent → LSP-ready
- `derivation-step` struct: 5 fields (`propagator-id` + `srcloc` + `assumption-ids` + `assumption-names` + `residual-cost`); transparent + minimal forward-compat surface
- `static-reverse-walk` primitive: builds O(N) reverse-index (cell→writers), DFS pre-order cons-onto-head, returns natural causal-reading order
- Cycle detection via prop-id visited set
- Bit-position → assumption-id decoding for tagged-cell-value entries (bit positions 0-29 per Phase 3A bit budget)
- Decomplection: primitive sets `assumption-names='()` + `residual-cost=#f`; consumers enrich
- Cell/Propagator/Scheduler Orthogonality preserved: NO `current-bsp-observer` activation; static walk over on-network state only

**Methodology data points captured**:

- **Mini-audit catches API mismatch pre-implementation** — verified Racket's built-in `srcloc` vs Prologos's 4-field variant via grep before write; missed this distinction in the test code → caught at runtime instead. **Sub-lesson**: when an upstream type has a same-name redefinition, the test code should explicitly verify shape via small probe before writing extensive test cases.
- **Atomic sub-phase commit (design + impl + tests) is honest when scope is small** — 3C.a's 156 + 165 LoC fits comfortably in one commit. The cleanliness of "one bisect point" outweighs the separate-design-commit discipline for small sub-phases.
- **3-column adversarial framing applied per Q-A.* at mini-design** — each Q's rationale captured catalogue (what passes) + challenge (could it be MORE aligned) + adversarial (what BREAKS under each option). User accepted leans without challenge → adversarial framing surfaced complete reasoning.

**Methodology codification candidates** (1 data point each, watching list):

- **Same-name type redefinition in dependency graph** — when an upstream module redefines a same-name type from a base library (e.g., Prologos's `srcloc` vs Racket's built-in), test code should grep the upstream redefinition shape before writing extensive tests. Codified at workflow.md or testing.md? TBD when 2nd data point lands.
- **Atomic sub-phase commit appropriate when scope < 200 LoC** — for foundation sub-phases that ship struct + primitive + tests together, atomic commit reduces overhead vs separate design+impl commits. Codified when 2nd data point validates.

### Phase 3C.b scope preview (per §9.5.1.5)

**3C.b — Union-contradict consumer** (~80-150 LoC):

1. NEW function `derivation-chain-for/union-contradict net aid-set request-info → derivation-chain`
2. Wraps `static-reverse-walk` from Phase 3C.a primitive — uses `#:filter-fn` to narrow walk to steps whose aids intersect the contradicted branch aid-set
3. ENRICHES steps with `assumption-names` via `solver-state-assumptions` lookup (using `current-command-atms` parameter)
4. OPTIONALLY ENRICHES with `residual-cost` via `tropical-left-residual` (UC3 reframed: per-step cost annotation against canonical fuel-cost-cell, NOT per-branch fuel cells)
5. INTEGRATION HOOK: augment `process-fork-contradiction` (typing-propagators.rkt:1288) — when narrowing would clear ALL branch bits (or some threshold-tbd configuration), build chain + emit error
6. Tests: inferred-union scenarios where Phase 3A's mechanism actually fires (different from probe baseline which uses annotated unions hitting sexp check/err)

**Key design questions for 3C.b mini-design** (the focused conversation at next session):
- **Q-B.1**: integration trigger — fire chain emission ONLY when ALL branches contradict, OR also for partial contradiction (some branches narrow, others survive)?
- **Q-B.2**: where does the chain go? Write to a new error-output cell (on-network), OR populate `union-exhaustion-error.derivation-chain` directly (return value path)?
- **Q-B.3**: filter-fn shape — `(step → bool)` predicate; consumer closes over branch aid-set? Or pass aid-set directly to the consumer wrapper?
- **Q-B.4**: residual-cost enrichment — ship in 3C.b OR defer to 3C.d? UC3's per-step cost annotation is a `tropical-left-residual` call against fuel-cost-cell-id — small scope but could defer.
- **Q-B.5**: test scenarios for inferred union — what `.prologos` syntax causes Phase 3A's mechanism to fire? Q6.x probe used annotated unions which don't trigger 3A; need different scenario.

4. **Same-name srcloc gotcha** (caught at 3C.a; watching-list codification):
   - Prologos's `source-location.rkt:27` defines `(struct srcloc (file line col span))` — 4 fields
   - Racket's built-in `srcloc` has 5 fields (file line col position span)
   - Test code must use 4-field variant via `(require "../source-location.rkt")` — DO NOT pass 5 args

5. **Phase 3A integration context** (for 3C.b integration site):
   - `process-fork-contradiction` at typing-propagators.rkt:1288 narrows worldview-cache when branches contradict

   - All-branch-narrowing condition: when `contradicted-bits` would clear all active branch bits from worldview-cache (after AND-with-NOT-mask)
   - The handler runs ATOMICALLY BETWEEN BSP rounds (not during) — chain emission piggybacks on the same atomicity

### Architectural refinement during dialogue (key methodology data point)

**User-articulated coupling intuition** rejected initial option (c) handler-side detection: "I'm concerned about the handler design, in general. It seems like there is a coupling happening here, and I don't have enough overview of the mechanism to assess exactly what feels off about it."

Articulated the coupling: option (c) makes `process-fork-on-union` + `process-fork-contradiction` MULTI-CONCERN (decomposition + worldview-narrowing + chain-emission mixed). **Decomplection violation; workflow.md "pragmatic shortcut" red flag in disguise.**

**Refined to option (d)**: per-fork threshold-fire-once propagator + per-position monotone latch cell-18. Existing handlers stay SINGLE-CONCERN; chain-emission gets its OWN propagator per fork; matches set-latch + threshold pattern from `.claude/rules/propagator-design.md` (canonical).

**Cascading simplification**: Q-B.1.iii (idempotence) STRUCTURALLY SUBSUMED by threshold-fire-once semantic — cell-19 emitted-chains tracker ELIMINATED. 3 cells reduce to 2 (cell-18 latch + cell-20 chain storage).

### Methodology data points captured

- **Audit + 3-column adversarial framing surfaces structural improvements user intuition catches but framing-discipline forces articulation** — option (c) → option (d) refinement happened DURING the 3-column analysis, not before. Confirms the third column ACTIVELY demolishes (not softly improves) when applied honestly. This session is concrete confirmation of the methodology refinement codified after PPN 4C S2.c-iii drift.
- **User-articulated architectural concerns are MORE LOAD-BEARING than my initial analysis** — I had option (d) on my list of alternatives but had REJECTED it for wrong reasons ("more propagators per fork" — actually a constant cost; "requires per-position latch cell anyway" — TRUE but it's the principled cell; "in-round emission complicates idempotence" — false, fire-once subsumes). The user's "something feels off" gave me permission to re-examine and find the real answer.
- **Cascading simplification** — when an architectural refinement IS principled, downstream questions often simplify or dissolve. Q-B.1.iii went from "needs cell-19 emitted-chains tracker" to "structurally subsumed by fire-once." (Methodology data point — 1 instance, watching-list candidate "principled architectural refinement cascades to downstream question simplification".)

### Codification candidates (NEW from 3C.b mini-design arc)

For future readers beyond 3C.b (Phase 9b γ; Phase 11b general), install a separate propagator that reads cell-16 and writes derived state; keep watcher fire-fn STABLE (2-cell fan-out is the load-bearing minimum). Promote at 2nd data point (Phase 9b γ adoption).
- **Cascading simplification from principled architectural refinement** — when option choice changes from "convenient" to "principled" (option c → option d), downstream questions often dissolve (Q-B.1.iii eliminated cell-19). Codify at 2nd data point.
- **User-articulated coupling intuition → audit re-examination** — when user says "something feels off" without articulating specifics, treat as methodology signal to re-examine the design with adversarial framing. The user's intuition has information; my initial 3-column might have been softened by implementation bias.
